To check Power Analysis Check

What we found

We detected 1 potential power analysis.

Show details

We used the LLM model ‘vllm/qwen3.5:4b’ to check the contents of 3 paragraphs that contained words suggesting they might contain power analyses.

Some essential information could not be detected: sample_size, effect_size, effect_size_metric

power_type statistical_test sample_size alpha_level power effect_size effect_size_metric software
posthoc other NA 0.05 0.34 NA NA other

You reported a power analysis that has been classified as ‘post-hoc’. Calculating observed power is almost never useful. If you actually performed a sensitivity power analysis, label it as such explicitly.

text
Continuous variables are given as median (interquartile range, IQR) and categorical variables as n (%). Groups were compared with the Mann-Whitney U test or Fisher’s exact test. All tests were twosided with α = 0.05. Comparisons in Tables 1 and 2 are descriptive and were not corrected for multiplici…

Cells longer than 300 characters are cut (…).

Power analyses need to contain the following information to be interpretable: the type of power analysis, the statistical test, the software used, sample size, critical alpha criterion, power level, effect size, and an effect size metric. In addition, it is recommended to make sure the power analysis is reproducible (by sharing the code, or a screenshot, of the power analysis), and to provide good arguments for why the study was designed to detect an effect of this size.

For an a-priori power analysis, where the sample size is determined, reporting all information would look like:

An a priori power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that for a Cohen’s d = 0.5, an alpha level of 0.05, and a desired power level of 80% required at least 64 participants in each group.

For a sensitivity power analysis, this sentence would look like:

A sensitivity power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that with 64 participants in each group, and an alpha level of 0.05, a desired power level of 80% was reached for an effect size of d = 0.5.

To check Non-Significant P Value Check

What we found

We found 2 non-significant p values that should be checked for appropriate interpretation.

Show details

Meta-scientific research has shown nonsignificant p values are commonly misinterpreted. It is incorrect to infer that there is ‘no effect’, ‘no difference’, or that groups are ‘the same’ after p > 0.05.

It is possible that there is a true non-zero effect, but that the study did not detect it. Make sure your inference acknowledges that it is possible that there is a non-zero effect. It is correct to include the effect is ‘not significantly’ different, although this just restates that p > 0.05.

Metacheck does not yet analyze automatically whether sentences which include non-significant p-values are correct, but we recommend manually checking the sentences below for possible misinterpreted non-significant p values.

Text Sentence
p = 0.095 In Kragujevac, a clinical model predicted severe outcome (AUC 0.84, 95% CI 0.75-0.92). rs11549465 CT/TT carriers had lower mortality (1/11 vs 28/78; p = 0.095).
p = 0.095 Mortality was 9.1% (1/11) in rs11549465 CT/TT carriers and 35.9% (28/78) in CC homozygotes (OR 0.18, exact 95% CI 0.004-1.39; p = 0.095).

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate of misinterpretations of non-significant results is high, see:

Aczel B, Palfi B, Szollosi A, Kovacs M, Szaszi B, Szecsi P, Zrubka M, Gronau Q, van den Bergh D, Wagenmakers E (2018). “Quantifying Support for the Null Hypothesis in Psychology: An Empirical Investigation.” Advances in Methods and Practices in Psychological Science, 1(3), 357–366. doi:10.1177/2515245918773742.

Murphy S, Merz R, Reimann L, Fernández A (2025). “Nonsignificance misinterpreted as an effect’s absence in psychology: Prevalence and temporal analyses.” Royal Society Open Science, 12(3), 242167. doi:10.1098/rsos.242167.

For educational material on preventing the misinterpretation of p values, see Improving Your Statistical Inferences.

Cells longer than 300 characters are cut (…).

Working Directory (setwd)

Best programming practice is to avoid setwd() in analysis code: it hardcodes an assumption about the working directory (often an absolute path on the author’s own machine), so the code breaks when run anywhere else. Keep the working directory as the caller sets it and use relative paths. No setwd() calls were found in any of the code files.

Package Installation (install.packages)

Best programming practice is to avoid install.packages() in analysis code: it installs software on whoever runs the script without asking, which is at minimum disruptive (an unexpected install, network access, a package version the author never tested against) and at worst a security risk if it points at an untrusted repository. Document required packages instead (a README, or a list of them: an renv.lock/DESCRIPTION for R, a requirements.txt for Python). No install.packages() calls were found in any of the code files.

Libraries / Imports

Best programming practice is to load all required libraries/imports in one block near the top of the code. In 20 code files, libraries/imports were at multiple places (i.e., with more than 3 non-comment lines in between).

Packages / Dependencies

The code files load 30 distinct packages/libraries: anndata, argparse, Bio, collections, covid_config, csv, datetime, docx, lightgbm, matplotlib, numpy, os, pandas, pathlib, pickle, PIL, re, requests, scanpy, scipy, seaborn, shap, shutil, sklearn, sys, time, urllib, warnings, wordcloud, xgboost. These are the names found in the source (no version information is available from static analysis).

File paths

19 data paths in the code do not work in the repository as deposited. We pointed them to where the file is, so the code could run: covid_lit_matrix.csv to ../results/raw_searches/covid_lit_matrix.csv (in build_s5_docx.py); covid_ratio_by_year.csv to ../results/raw_searches/covid_ratio_by_year.csv (in build_s5_docx.py); query_validation_counts.csv to ../results/raw_searches/query_validation_counts.csv (in build_s5_docx.py); top_terms.csv to ../results/topic_analysis/top_terms.csv (in build_s5_docx.py); topic_clusters.csv to ../results/topic_analysis/topic_clusters.csv (in build_s5_docx.py); covid_lit_matrix.csv to ../results/raw_searches/covid_lit_matrix.csv (in covid_figures.py); covid_ratio_by_year.csv to ../results/raw_searches/covid_ratio_by_year.csv (in covid_figures.py); query_validation_counts.csv to ../results/raw_searches/query_validation_counts.csv (in covid_prisma.py); feature_names.pkl to scale_c_sirio_brazil/models/feature_names.pkl (in 15_scale_c_polish.py); scaler.pkl to scale_c_sirio_brazil/models/scaler.pkl (in 15_scale_c_polish.py), and 9 more. The code should be changed, because as written it does not find these files. The code reads files that are only inside zivanovicmkg/hif1a-covid-multiscale-v1.0.0.zip, and no script unpacks it. We unpacked it in its folder before the run. So that others can run the code as deposited, start the code with a line that unpacks it (such as zipfile.ZipFile("hif1a-covid-multiscale-v1.0.0.zip").extractall() in Python), or upload the files unpacked.

Ethics Approval Check

The paper appears to analyse records about individual people that the authors obtained for this study, and it has an ethics statement.

We based this on:

Scale D used prospectively collected clinical data and blood samples from patients hospitalized at the University Clinical Center Kragujevac, Serbia, within the study “Investigation of the humoral response and of phenotypic and functional features of peripheral blood cells in patients with COVID-19”, approved by the Ethics Committee of the Clinical Center Kragujevac (No.

Scales A-C used de-identified public data released under licences that permit secondary analysis.

Author contributions Conceptualization: Marko N. Živanović, Ivana Raković, Biljana Popovska Jovičić; Methodology: Marko N. Živanović; Software: Marko N. Živanović; Formal analysis: Marko N. Živanović, Miloš Z. Milosavljević; Investigation (patient recruitment and clinical data): Ivana Raković, Biljana Popovska Jovičić, Miloš Z. Živanović (computational resources), Ivana Raković, Biljana Popovska Jovičić (clinical resources); Data curation: Marko N. Živanović; Visualization: Marko N. Živanović; Project administration: Marko N. Živanović; Ivana Raković, Biljana Popovska Jovičić (ethics approval); Supervision: Marko N. Živanović; Funding acquisition: Marko N. Ethics statement:

Scale D used prospectively collected clinical data and blood samples from patients hospitalized at the University Clinical Center Kragujevac, Serbia, within the study “Investigation of the humoral response and of phenotypic and functional features of peripheral blood cells in patients with COVID-19”, approved by the Ethics Committee of the Clinical Center Kragujevac (No.

The study was conducted in accordance with the Declaration of Helsinki, and all participants gave written informed consent.

This module runs automated data-quality checks (empty columns, SPSS filter variables, category casing, stray whitespace, numbers stored as text, problematic and colliding column names, mixed text encodings, personal information, spreadsheet formatting, and careless responding) on the extracted data files.

We examined 508 columns across 90 data files.

Careless Responding

No file could be screened for careless responding.

What this does not cover. Only 0 of 90 data files could be screened: the rest had no detectable multi-item scale block, or too few respondents. Even in a screened file, these checks only look at multi-item rating scales — the only place where a repeated or flat answer pattern is interpretable. Single questions, open text, demographics and any item outside a detected scale block are never examined. A respondent can therefore answer most of a survey carelessly and still not be flagged here, and finding nothing is not evidence that a dataset is free of careless responding.
